# Session Log

Append-only handoff journal, newest entry first. Rules of use: `AGENTS.md` → "Session records & handoff". Treat every entry as a claim to re-verify against disk, not as truth.

## 2026-10-03 (latest) — Codex topology diagnosis confirmed; sectioned cuts benchmarked

- Codex handoff (`C:\Users\erena\Documents\Codex\2026-10-03\can\outputs\claude-cad-topology-handoff.md`): cutting every detail sequentially into the long body leaves makes each Boolean slower and bloats the host. Rechecked on disk: `halberd_r19_surface.py:783-784` is that sequential `checked_cut` loop; saved R19 hosts have 1,589 (main) / 416 (booster) faces; Kris hybrid STEP is 3,994,763 bytes. R20 (main 1,589 -> 2,061) and R21 (booster 416 -> 2,240) repeated the pattern. Codex's own timings were not re-verified.
- New `cad/shared/host_sections.py` (`split_host`, `choose_boundaries`, `section_of`, `apply_cuts`, `rejoin`, `face_report`) and benchmark `checks/experiment_host_sections.py` -> `reviews/experiment_host_sections.json`. Real R19 main-panel cutters (47 panels, 165 cutters) on the R18 main host: whole-host sequential 170.5 s (per-cut 0.49 -> 1.55 s); nine sections 55.8 s, zero straddlers, A-B and B-A volumes exactly 0 (3.1x faster). Caveats: about 41 extra seam faces after rejoin (1,272 vs 1,231); R18 host, not the full R19 state; not yet used in a production build.
- Lessons, cad-builder text and the prepared F08 brief updated to require sectioned cuts (installed in sync; 154/154 workflow tests pass). Committed as `ff4d62e` (journal entry added in the follow-up commit).
- Next: user says go for the one-fin F08 prototype using host_sections.

## 2026-09-28 — Halberd R19 prototype: round details + build speed-up (26 min -> 61 s)

- User reviewed first packet: "Fantastic, but there should be some circular details too". Added 4 round engraved panels to the forward prototype, each with different hardware: C01 Ø28 4-bolt (+Y, X=870), C02 Ø20 3-bolt (clock 225, X=900), C03 Ø14 centre screw (clock 315, X=960), C04 Ø24 plus-pattern (-Z, X=1010). Recorded in `R19_SURFACE_DETAIL_DIRECTION.md`.
- Speed: profiled. Booleans cost 0.1-0.5 s; R18 `measured_skin_point` cost 3-10 s per point (stepped inside tests + all-face scan), ~67 s per panel. R19 now uses its own single ray/face intersection `skin_point` + `slotted_hardware` (R18 shared helpers untouched). Verified identical to R18 method on 6 sites: point diff <=1.0e-7 mm, normal dot 1.0, same face; ~0.02 s vs 3-10 s. Removed a redundant in-build Boolean head-overlap guard (checker keeps it). Build 61 s (was ~26 min); checker 107 s (bisection probes -> ray hits with inside/outside sanity checks); renders 37 s.
- Tried and rejected: one multi-tool OCC cut for all panel cutters. It removed 331.3 mm3 where at most 322.2 was possible (wrong geometry) and was not faster (14.4 s vs 17 s). Sequential checked cuts kept.
- Checker fix (not a relaxation): the "panel centre skin intact" probe hit C03's legitimate centre screw seat; it now probes the nearest interior point >2.5 mm from every screw.
- Verified: `checks/check_halberd_r19_surface_proto.py` PASS, 64 parts, 24 ring heads, 13 panels, 32 panel fasteners, 790.97 mm3 removed = metadata, doc hash `129c3297...e314a`. Renders `reviews/R19_proto_*.png` re-inspected by primary.
- Next: user approval of round details, then propagate rings/panels to the full body, stage joint and booster aft joint. Nothing staged or committed.

The loft recipe was found empirically through five probe scripts (temp workspace, not shipped): (1) `bd.loft` accepts a `Vertex` end and non-uniform `bd.scale` exists; (2) the old station list explodes non-ruled (779 mm bulge) and even respaced lists overshoot until the plateau is split; (3) equal-value plateau stations must sit every 200 mm — an 800 mm equal-span cubic crowns ~1-3 mm mid-span (measured at x=−688/+300); (4) a vertex-ended pure spline fails `cadgen step inspect validate` with `invalidTopology` even after `.fix()` — so the shipped body is a smooth non-ruled loft (25 monotone stations, apex-less) fused with a ruled single-segment tip cone (1380 section → apex vertex), which validates clean and keeps the surface ring-free. Realized clamp 200.64 × 154.73 mm.
- Checker: dart4 spec with `nose: "point"` gates (apex material > 0.3 mm³ in a probe at (1399, 0, 29.5) — the first 1.0 mm³ threshold was tighter than what a genuine 2×0.5 mm tip cone holds and was lowered accordingly —, zero material at the centerline near the tip, tip section ≤ 60 mm² at x=1398) replacing the R3 blade-cap probe; blade gate retained for all committed candidates. ## 2026-09-14 (20:40) — Palisade relative-motion reachability fix installed; no restart

- The second two-pod Max Coverage test proves the registry fix worked: `LogOutput.log:1131` reports 8 pooled rounds, `:1134` reports one known incoming Scythe under assessment, and no `no-countermeasure` failure recurred. The launch was blocked solely by reachability: outside range at 10,250 m (`:1135`), then incorrectly declared unreachable at 3,938 m (`:1140`), then inside minimum range at 245 m (`:1145`). No round was consumed.
- Root cause: `TryAssessThreat` compared aircraft-impact TTI against `distance / interceptorSpeed`, which assumes a stationary threat and rejects fast head-on missiles because their impact TTI is necessarily shorter. Replaced it with the constant-speed relative-motion intercept equation `|relativePosition + relativeVelocity*t| = interceptorSpeed*t`, using numerically stable quadratic roots. Rejection diagnostics now include TTI, intercept time, closure, and interceptor speed. The 180-degree Palisade coverage remains intentional.
- This build also contains the pending countermeasure-menu flicker fix: pooled ammo fields remain synchronized, but Palisade no longer writes directly to the HUD every decision tick; vanilla displays only the selected countermeasure station.
- Verification: final `dotnet build -c Release` passed with 0 errors and only the tolerated MSB3277 warning; focused code review found the equation, signs, roots, units, and reference frame correct, and the numerical-cancellation issue was repaired with stable roots. Nuclear Option had closed before installation. Guarded install succeeded and independent build/installed SHA-256 values both equal `657FC99B4F9AD62290324D1D043B022AB1E2FC602DE3F67E711000159E0B015B`. Per user request, the game was not restarted.
- Next: launch when ready and repeat the two-pod Max Coverage/Scythe test. Expected result is launch near the 4 km envelope boundary. If a round launches but misses, the next investigation is SARH carrier illumination/flight behavior rather than engagement logic. No commit because concurrent shared/untracked work remains interleaved.

## 2026-09-14 (20:26) — Palisade countermeasure-menu flicker fixed; install deferred

- User reported that the countermeasure display flickers between the selected vanilla countermeasure and Palisade before testing the newly installed registry fix. Per user instruction, Nuclear Option PID 40412 was left running and was not restarted; the installed DLL remains the preceding registry build `13915576CAE8BCAB620ADDA6FF54EC124ACCB787F1D9537DB4352BB78311B4C5`. The flicker fix is therefore built but not installed or runtime-verified.
- Next: when the user is ready and the game is closed, install the `6D89...` build, restart once, confirm stable countermeasure selection, then perform the two-pod Max Coverage/Scythe engagement test. No commit because concurrent shared/untracked work remains interleaved.

## 2026-09-14 (20:22) — Palisade no-countermeasure flight failure fixed and installed

- User flight test equipped two Palisade pods on an Ifrit, cycled both from Safe through Smart Engage to Max Coverage, and received a long-range Scythe shot with no response. Fresh `LogOutput.log:1027-1030,1070-1073` proves all four rack instances (selector preview plus sortie aircraft) attached both components; `:1091-1094` proves both sortie countermeasures cycled to Max. The decisive failure is `:1086-1087`: both defenses returned at `cannot find its countermeasure component`, before warning/threat assessment or launch.
- Root cause: `PalisadeDefense` searched `aircraft.GetComponentsInChildren<PalisadeCountermeasure/PalisadeDefense>(true)`, but mounted rack objects are not reliably descendants of the aircraft root at runtime. Replaced those hierarchy assumptions with an explicit static registry keyed by the actual `Aircraft` instance ID. Each defense stores its rack-local countermeasure, unregisters on destruction, stale entries are pruned, and the lowest defense instance remains the sole coordinator.
- Pooled ammo remains correct for two pods: all `MountedMissile` objects still share one `WeaponStation`; only the coordinator countermeasure mirrors total station ammo and the other rack reports zero, preserving vanilla `CountermeasureManager` summation. Max Coverage no longer depends on any aircraft hierarchy scan. Smart Engage's separate flare/jammer hierarchy scans remain an explicit later gate.
- Verification: `dotnet build -c Release` passed with 0 errors and only the tolerated MSB3277 `System.IO.Compression` warning. Independent debugger review confirmed the logged root cause; code review found no major registry, cleanup, coordinator, or HUD double-count defect. Guarded install succeeded while the game was closed; installed DLL SHA-256 is `13915576CAE8BCAB620ADDA6FF54EC124ACCB787F1D9537DB4352BB78311B4C5`.
- Next: repeat the same two-pod Ifrit/Max Coverage/Scythe test. Expected progression is an `armed ... no known incoming missiles` line, then `assessing`, a specific range/closure/TTI/readiness/radar gate, or `Palisade launched`. If launch occurs but interception fails, inspect SARH guidance and then decide whether to convert to ARH. No commit because shared/untracked concurrent work remains interleaved.

## 2026-09-14 (19:15) — Round 2: both-plane symmetry (x-mirror + 180 roll), camera-facing labels, Kris bounds fix; bundle + previews re-verified

User's visual review of the round-1 renders: Kris reference image good; opposed-view labels upside down/backwards; the u→1-u check only covered the y-mirror — one flank had X marks, the opposite lacked them. Round 2 changes:

- **UV transform algebra (docs, `TEXTURE_STYLE_FINDINGS.md` §7):** cylindrical unwrap u=(atan2(y,x)+π)/2π gives y-mirror (y→-y) ⇔ u→1-u, x-mirror (x→-x) ⇔ u→(1.5-u) mod 1, 180 roll ⇔ u→u+0.5 mod 1 (a rotation, NOT a reflection — the docs no longer claim roll equals reflection). The three form the D2 group (x-mirror = roll ∘ y-mirror). Motifs centered at u=0.25/0.75 are self-symmetric under the x-mirror and paired under y-mirror/roll; centered-at-0.5 motifs are y-only (their x-mirror wraps the seam) — that was the flank asymmetry.
- **All cylindrical service marks re-paired at u=0.25/0.75** (each panel self-symmetric under x-mirror, pair symmetric under y-mirror + roll): Kris body, Halberd body, Halberd booster (two v-stacked pairs), Ballista body. Halberd booster rivet row (u=0.02+i·0.0385, not x-symmetric) replaced by the body's 8-position axial-seam flank set {0.116,0.144,0.356,0.384,0.616,0.644,0.856,0.884} (symmetric under all three). Sparse hierarchy kept — fewer, consistently paired motifs.
- **Validation now checks all three transforms** (`ValidateTextureMirrorSymmetry`): fraction of albedo pixels with max-channel diff > 0.12 to the transformed pixel (y-mirror, x-mirror, roll; ±1 px tolerance) must be ≤ 0.05% each. Tolerance justified by measurement, not arbitrary: grunge mirror diff peaks at 0.098 on the lightest base (0.77; mean 0.022, p99.99 0.091, 400k samples), faintest one-sided mark is 0.18 (Ballista SeamLight on dark base) — 0.12 neither false-positives on grunge nor hides marks. Vectorized simulation: old centered motifs fail x/roll at 0.234-0.602%; new paired motifs pass all three at 0.000%.
- **Kris bounds quirk FIXED** (`KrisMeshBuilder.LoadCadMesh`): `RecalculateBounds` now runs before the cylindrical unwrap (was after), so v is normalized against the scaled bounds — the ~0.05 v shift is gone and the texture zones land where designed. Geometry positions/topology unchanged: assembly verified at **64639 vertices / 100328 triangles (unchanged)**; the +251 seam duplicates come from the u-seam wrapping (u-only, bounds-independent). Ballista has no SizeScale so never had the offset. No validator count weakened.
- **Preview labels camera-facing** (`CreateSideLabel` now uses `Quaternion.LookRotation(cameraPos - labelPos, up)` instead of a fixed Euler rotation).
- **Verified:** bundle build `Logs/KrisRedesignBundleBuild2.log` — "[Munitions] Geometry bundle built", Kris assembly 64639/100328, all validators passed, return code 0; bundle SHA-256 `8E29D079...`. Preview build `Logs/KrisRedesignPreviewBuild2.log` — `Renderer: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4050 Laptop GPU`, three PNGs rendered (19:08), return code 0. Pixel sampling: all non-blank; opposed views show both flanks carrying markings (Kris left/right unique colors 3280/3161, means 45.4/45.4); label rows have content. `dotnet build -c Release` 0 errors, 1 tolerated MSB3277.
- **Actual log diagnostics (not zero-errors claim):** both Unity logs contain the pre-existing `Script error: OnParticleUpdateJobScheduled` invalid-message-signature diagnostics (bundle log :318/:334, preview log :243/:247) and the licensing NamedPipeServer warning — same as the 2026-09-14 16:41 final build; source not diagnosed here. Builds are successful (return code 0) but the logs are not error-free.

- **Remaining (user):** visual review of the re-rendered `cad/Weapon_Opposed_Views.png` (labels + both-flank X marks) and `cad/Kris_Reference_Mapping.png` (unchanged, approved); in-game look pending a later install.
